Western Workplace & Life Cases — 384 Lines
001 Yao H01 Qian Line 1▶
001爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H01-L1 | XIANG-U-01-02

Historical event Cincinnatus returns to his farm. Rome faced a military emergency and appointed Lucius Quinctius Cincinnatus dictator, an extraordinary office with broad authority. Ancient tradition presents him as a landowner who had been away from ordinary public office when the summons came. He accepted the appointment, organized the Roman response, and after the emergency relinquished the dictatorship rather than keeping exceptional power. He then returned to private life and agricultural work. The episode was later retold as an example of temporary service rather than permanent possession of office. The historical details come through later Roman writers and should not be treated as a complete contemporary record. Even so, the sequence is clear in the tradition: urgent public duty interrupted private work, the task was completed, and the office was surrendered. His return to the farm became part of his long afterlife in European and American civic culture. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Line-specific comparison The hidden dragon places capability below public authority. For a workplace or life decision, this line is useful before the visible move begins. Set beside Cincinnatus returns to his farm, the image becomes concrete rather than abstract. Cincinnatus returns to his farm. Rome faced a military emergency and appointed Lucius Quinctius Cincinnatus dictator, an extraordinary office with broad authority.. The decision changed duties, relationships, resources, or the direction of work; it did not guarantee an easy outcome. Creative capacity matters, but finishing a demanding assignment can include knowing when to release authority and return to ordinary life. The case of Cincinnatus returns. For this line, timing, fit, responsibility, restraint, or follow-through should be judged at the stage represented here, not from the final result alone. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented. What must exist before a professional claim becomes credible? This is a present-day editorial comparison. The historical reference documents the event, not the participant's use of this line or the Ten Wings.

Evidence: RETROSPECTIVE ANALOGY. Source confirms the workplace/life-decision event only. Historical reference ↗

Historical case EUUS-WL-H02: Augustine leaves a rhetoric career · Milan and North Africa, Europe · 386–391 CE

007 Yao H02 Kun Line 1▶
007爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H02-L1 | XIANG-U-02-02

Historical event Augustine leaves a rhetoric career. Augustine had built a career as a teacher of rhetoric and eventually held a prestigious teaching post in Milan. His work depended on education, public speaking, and the patronage networks of the late Roman world. During the 380s he withdrew from that professional path, was baptized in Milan in 387, and returned to North Africa. He formed a religious community, was ordained a priest at Hippo, and later became bishop. The transition replaced a career centered on rhetoric with one centered on pastoral, intellectual, and institutional work. He continued writing and teaching for decades, but within a different vocation and set of responsibilities. His later books drew on skills developed in his earlier education while serving a new audience and purpose. The decision did not erase the first career; it redirected capabilities acquired there into a different form of work and life. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Line-specific comparison Frost precedes hard ice through an accumulating process. For a workplace or life decision, this line is useful before the visible move begins. Set beside Augustine leaves a rhetoric career, the image becomes concrete rather than abstract. Augustine leaves a rhetoric career. Augustine had built a career as a teacher of rhetoric and eventually held a prestigious teaching post in Milan.. The decision changed duties, relationships, resources, or the direction of work; it did not guarantee an easy outcome. A receptive decision is not passive. Sometimes a person stops leading with the old plan, listens to a deeper constraint, and lets existing skills serve. For this line, timing, fit, responsibility, restraint, or follow-through should be judged at the stage represented here, not from the final result alone. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented. Which small pressures are accumulating into a larger problem? This is a present-day editorial comparison. The historical reference documents the event, not the participant's use of this line or the Ten Wings.

012 Yao H02 Kun Line 6▶
012爻 Line 6 | TW-WL-H02-L6 | XIANG-U-02-07

Historical event Augustine leaves a rhetoric career. Augustine had built a career as a teacher of rhetoric and eventually held a prestigious teaching post in Milan. His work depended on education, public speaking, and the patronage networks of the late Roman world. During the 380s he withdrew from that professional path, was baptized in Milan in 387, and returned to North Africa. He formed a religious community, was ordained a priest at Hippo, and later became bishop. The transition replaced a career centered on rhetoric with one centered on pastoral, intellectual, and institutional work. He continued writing and teaching for decades, but within a different vocation and set of responsibilities. His later books drew on skills developed in his earlier education while serving a new audience and purpose. The decision did not erase the first career; it redirected capabilities acquired there into a different form of work and life. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H03: Benedict establishes Monte Cassino · Italy, Europe · c. 529 CE

013 Yao H03 Zhun Line 1▶
013爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H03-L1 | XIANG-U-03-02

Historical event Benedict establishes Monte Cassino. Benedict of Nursia lived during a period of political fragmentation in Italy. Religious communities already existed, but rules, leadership practices, and daily routines varied widely. He established a monastery at Monte Cassino and is associated with the Rule of Saint Benedict, which organized prayer, reading, manual labor, obedience, hospitality, and community life. The rule treated daily work as part of a disciplined common rhythm rather than as an activity separate from spiritual life. Benedictine communities later spread across Europe and adapted the rule to different places and centuries. The monastery became not simply a retreat but a durable institution with roles, schedules, shared resources, and procedures for leadership. The case shows how an uncertain beginning can become workable when ordinary routines are specified and repeated. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. Historical case EUUS-WL-H04: Hildegard founds Rupertsberg · German lands, Europe · c. 1150

019 Yao H04 Meng Line 1▶
019爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H04-L1 | XIANG-U-04-02

Historical event Hildegard founds Rupertsberg. Hildegard of Bingen became a leader within a religious community at Disibodenberg, where women lived under arrangements connected to a male monastery. As her community and reputation grew, the existing setting no longer matched all of its needs. She pursued the creation of a separate convent at Rupertsberg near Bingen. The move required ecclesiastical approval, property, construction, funding, and the transfer of a community. From the new base she managed correspondence, music, writing, religious duties, and relationships with patrons and church authorities. Rupertsberg became the center of her later work, and she eventually founded another house at Eibingen. The change illustrates a practical workplace decision inside a medieval institution: seeking a structure with greater operational independence while retaining obligations to a wider network. The result was not immediate freedom from constraint, but a new platform for sustained work. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. Historical case EUUS-WL-H05: Gutenberg builds a printing enterprise · Mainz, Europe · 1450s

025 Yao H05 Xu Line 1▶
025爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H05-L1 | XIANG-U-05-02

Historical event Gutenberg builds a printing enterprise. Johannes Gutenberg worked in a world where books were copied by hand or produced with labor-intensive methods. His printing project required more than one invention: movable metal type, ink, presses, skilled labor, paper, and substantial financing had to work together. In Mainz he entered financial arrangements with Johann Fust and developed a workshop capable of producing large printed works, including the Bible associated with his name. The enterprise required repeated experiments and expensive equipment before revenue could be realized. A later legal dispute with Fust affected control of the workshop and its assets. Printing nevertheless spread rapidly through European cities after the 1450s. Gutenberg did not personally control the entire industry that followed, but the system demonstrated that standardized text production could be organized as a repeatable business process. The case is a reminder that waiting during development can be active work when the product requires integration before launch. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. Historical case EUUS-WL-H06: Michelangelo accepts the Sistine ceiling commission · Rome, Europe · 1508–1512

031 Yao H06 Song Line 1▶
031爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H06-L1 | XIANG-U-06-02

Historical event Michelangelo accepts the Sistine ceiling commission. Michelangelo was known primarily as a sculptor when Pope Julius II commissioned him to paint the ceiling of the Sistine Chapel. The assignment was large, technically difficult, and outside the medium for which he had built his strongest reputation. He accepted the work and organized scaffolding, assistants, materials, and fresco production over several years. He revised the working process as the project developed and completed a vast sequence of figures across the ceiling. The commission interrupted other work and placed him inside a demanding patron relationship. The finished ceiling became one of the best-known works of Renaissance art and altered Michelangelo’s professional identity, making painting a central part of how later audiences understood him. The episode was not free of disagreement or pressure, but it shows a career decision in which conflict with role expectations did not prevent a person from completing work beyond the boundaries of an earlier specialty. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. Historical case EUUS-WL-H07: Leonardo moves to Milan · Milan, Europe · c. 1482

037 Yao H07 Shi Line 1▶
037爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H07-L1 | XIANG-U-07-02

Historical event Leonardo moves to Milan. Leonardo da Vinci trained and worked in Florence, but opportunities there were shared among many established artists and workshops. In the early 1480s he moved to Milan and entered the service of Ludovico Sforza. His work in Milan extended beyond painting. He designed court entertainments, studied engineering and anatomy, proposed military devices, and worked on projects including The Last Supper and a large equestrian monument that was never completed. Service at court required him to combine artistic ability with technical, organizational, and representational tasks. The Milan period lasted many years and broadened the range of Leonardo’s professional practice. Political change later forced him to leave, showing that a career built around a patron could also be vulnerable to the patron’s circumstances. The move nevertheless demonstrates how a deliberate change of environment can expand the range of work available to a skilled person. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H08: Boulton and Watt form a partnership · Birmingham, Britain · 1775

043 Yao H08 Bi Line 1▶
043爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H08-L1 | XIANG-U-08-02

Historical event Boulton and Watt form a partnership. James Watt had developed improvements to the steam engine but faced the usual difficulties of turning an invention into a reliable commercial product. Building engines required capital, manufacturing capability, customers, installation, and maintenance. Matthew Boulton brought an established manufacturing base at Soho and experience in business organization. Their partnership combined Watt’s engineering work with Boulton’s production resources and commercial network. They licensed and supplied engines for mines and factories while continuing to improve designs and manage technical problems in the field. The partnership became one of the best-known industrial collaborations of the eighteenth century. Its success depended less on a single inspired moment than on complementary roles maintained over time. The case illustrates workplace alliance: a specialist and an operator creating more value together because each supplied capabilities the other lacked. 044爻 Line 2 | TW-WL-H08-L2 | XIANG-U-08-03

Historical event Boulton and Watt form a partnership. James Watt had developed improvements to the steam engine but faced the usual difficulties of turning an invention into a reliable commercial product. Building engines required capital, manufacturing capability, customers, installation, and maintenance. Matthew Boulton brought an established manufacturing base at Soho and experience in business organization. Their partnership combined Watt’s engineering work with Boulton’s production resources and commercial network. They licensed and supplied engines for mines and factories while continuing to improve designs and manage technical problems in the field. The partnership became one of the best-known industrial collaborations of the eighteenth century. Its success depended less on a single inspired moment than on complementary roles maintained over time. The case illustrates workplace alliance: a specialist and an operator creating more value together because each supplied capabilities the other lacked. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H09: Benjamin Franklin establishes himself as a printer · Philadelphia, North America · 1723–1730s

049 Yao H09 Xiao Xu Line 1▶
049爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H09-L1 | XIANG-U-09-02

Historical event Benjamin Franklin establishes himself as a printer. Benjamin Franklin left his apprenticeship in Boston as a young man and arrived in Philadelphia with limited resources. Printing was a skilled trade that required equipment, customers, credit, and reputation, none of which could be created instantly. He worked for other printers, traveled to London, returned to Philadelphia, and eventually entered business on his own. He built a printing shop, published the Pennsylvania Gazette, produced Poor Richard’s Almanack, and developed relationships through civic and commercial activity. The growth was incremental and depended on repeated small decisions about work, saving, partnerships, and public reliability. The printing business gave Franklin financial independence and a platform for later scientific and civic work. His path was not a straight promotion inside one employer; it was a sequence of apprenticeships, moves, experiments, and accumulated credibility. The case shows how modest gains can compound before a career appears established from the outside. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. Historical case EUUS-WL-H10: Charles Darwin accepts the Beagle voyage · Britain and Atlantic world · 1831–1836

055 Yao H10 Lu Line 1▶
055爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H10-L1 | XIANG-U-10-02

Historical event Charles Darwin accepts the Beagle voyage. After university, Charles Darwin had not settled into a conventional profession. An opportunity arose to join HMS Beagle as a gentleman companion and naturalist on a surveying voyage, but the journey promised years away from home and no guaranteed career outcome. Darwin accepted after family discussion and sailed in 1831. During the voyage he collected specimens, recorded geological observations, corresponded with scientists, and encountered environments across South America and the Pacific. The work required careful routines of note-taking, preservation, comparison, and shipment back to Britain. When he returned in 1836, the collections and observations helped establish his scientific reputation. Years of further research followed before he published On the Origin of Species in 1859. The initial decision did not produce an immediate breakthrough; it placed him on a path where disciplined observation created options that had not existed before the voyage. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical event Bell Labs organizes the transistor team. Telephone networks needed more reliable and efficient electronic components than vacuum tubes could provide for many applications. Bell Labs had the resources to pursue fundamental physics and practical engineering in the same organization. A team including John Bardeen, Walter Brattain, and William Shockley worked on semiconductor problems. In 1947 Bardeen and Brattain demonstrated a working point-contact transistor, while Shockley later developed the junction transistor concept. The work involved theory, experiments, measurement, fabrication, and institutional support rather than a solitary invention. The transistor became a foundational technology for modern electronics. The individual relationships within the team later became strained, but the initial research environment demonstrated the value of assembling complementary expertise around a shared technical problem. Historical event Bell Labs organizes the transistor team. Telephone networks needed more reliable and efficient electronic components than vacuum tubes could provide for many applications. Bell Labs had the resources to pursue fundamental physics and practical engineering in the same organization. A team including John Bardeen, Walter Brattain, and William Shockley worked on semiconductor problems. In 1947 Bardeen and Brattain demonstrated a working point-contact transistor, while Shockley later developed the junction transistor concept. The work involved theory, experiments, measurement, fabrication, and institutional support rather than a solitary invention. The transistor became a foundational technology for modern electronics. The individual relationships within the team later became strained, but the initial research environment demonstrated the value of assembling complementary expertise around a shared technical problem. Historical case EUUS-WL-H12: Kodak develops but delays digital photography · New York, United States · 1975 onward

067 Yao H12 Pi Line 1▶
067爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H12-L1 | XIANG-U-12-02

Historical event Kodak develops but delays digital photography. Eastman Kodak dominated film photography when engineer Steven Sasson was asked to explore a new solid-state imaging technology. In 1975 he built an early self-contained digital camera prototype using a CCD sensor and digital storage. The prototype showed that photographs could be captured without film, but the image quality, speed, storage, and display ecosystem were still primitive. Kodak continued research and obtained important digital-imaging patents while its existing business remained tied to film, processing, and photographic paper. Over subsequent decades competitors and new consumer electronics companies advanced digital cameras and later camera phones. Kodak eventually sold digital products, but the transition undermined the economics of its historical core business. The company filed for Chapter 11 bankruptcy protection in 2012 and later emerged as a smaller business. The case is useful as a workplace decision story because recognizing a new technology and reorganizing around it are different tasks. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. Historical case EUUS-WL-H13: Hewlett and Packard build a company partnership · California, United States · 1939 onward

073 Yao H13 Tong Ren Line 1▶
073爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H13-L1 | XIANG-U-13-02

Historical event Hewlett and Packard build a company partnership. Bill Hewlett and David Packard met as engineering students at Stanford and later explored the idea of building electronic instruments. They started a small business in a rented garage in Palo Alto in 1939. Their early work included an audio oscillator that found a customer in Walt Disney Studios. As the company grew, the founders developed management practices that emphasized technical competence, decentralized responsibility, and respect for employees. HP expanded from instruments into computing and other technologies over several decades. The company’s later history included major restructurings and splits, but its early partnership became an influential example of Silicon Valley company building. The key decision was not merely to start a business; it was to sustain a working relationship in which technical and managerial responsibilities could be shared rather than concentrated in one founder. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. Historical event Hewlett and Packard build a company partnership. Bill Hewlett and David Packard met as engineering students at Stanford and later explored the idea of building electronic instruments. They started a small business in a rented garage in Palo Alto in 1939. Their early work included an audio oscillator that found a customer in Walt Disney Studios. As the company grew, the founders developed management practices that emphasized technical competence, decentralized responsibility, and respect for employees. HP expanded from instruments into computing and other technologies over several decades. The company’s later history included major restructurings and splits, but its early partnership became an influential example of Silicon Valley company building. The key decision was not merely to start a business; it was to sustain a working relationship in which technical and managerial responsibilities could be shared rather than concentrated in one founder. Historical case EUUS-WL-H14: Andrew Carnegie sells Carnegie Steel · United States · 1901

079 Yao H14 Da You Line 1▶
079爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H14-L1 | XIANG-U-14-02

Historical event Andrew Carnegie sells Carnegie Steel. Andrew Carnegie had built one of the largest steel businesses in the United States through investment in plants, technology, transportation, and aggressive cost control. By the end of the nineteenth century he was extremely wealthy and still associated with a demanding industrial enterprise. In 1901 J. P. Morgan organized the purchase of Carnegie Steel and combined it with other businesses to form U.S. Steel. Carnegie left active steel management with a fortune that made him one of the richest individuals of his era. He then devoted much of his remaining life to philanthropy, funding libraries, education, research, and foundations. The sale changed both his professional identity and the use of his resources. It did not erase controversies surrounding labor relations in the steel industry, including the Homestead conflict. As a life decision, the case shows that possessing more resources creates a second question: whether to keep maximizing the activity that created them or redirect them toward a different purpose. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H15: George Washington Carver joins Tuskegee · Alabama, United States · 1896 onward

085 Yao H15 Qian Line 1▶
085爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H15-L1 | XIANG-U-15-02

Historical event George Washington Carver joins Tuskegee. George Washington Carver trained in agriculture and earned a graduate degree at Iowa State Agricultural College. In 1896 Booker T. Washington invited him to lead agricultural work at Tuskegee Institute in Alabama. Carver accepted and spent much of his career at Tuskegee, teaching, conducting experiments, and producing practical bulletins for farmers. His work emphasized crop rotation, soil improvement, and uses for crops such as peanuts and sweet potatoes. Facilities and resources were limited compared with major research institutions, so he often worked with modest equipment and materials. Carver became nationally known, although popular stories later exaggerated some claims about his inventions. His long tenure shows a career decision shaped by mission and service rather than by the most prestigious laboratory setting. He used scientific training in an institution where education, extension work, and community needs were closely connected. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. 091 Yao H16 Yu Line 1▶
091爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H16-L1 | XIANG-U-16-02

Historical event Walt Disney commits to Snow White. By the mid-1930s Walt Disney’s studio was successful with animated shorts, but a feature-length animated film had not yet been proven as a commercial format in Hollywood. Producing one would require years of work, new artistic methods, training, and large financial commitments. Disney committed the studio to Snow White and the Seven Dwarfs. Costs rose as the project expanded, and the company had to manage thousands of drawings, music, story development, effects, and production schedules. The film premiered in 1937 and was released widely in 1938. Its commercial success gave the studio resources and credibility for additional feature films and a larger production organization. The decision also increased the scale and complexity of the workplace. Enthusiasm created momentum, but the result depended on turning excitement into systems that could coordinate many specialized workers over a long project. Historical case EUUS-WL-H17: Intel exits the memory-chip business · California, United States · 1980s

097 Yao H17 Sui Line 1▶
097爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H17-L1 | XIANG-U-17-02

Historical event Intel exits the memory-chip business. Intel had been built around semiconductor memory, but by the early 1980s Japanese competitors were putting intense pressure on the memory market. At the same time, microprocessors were becoming a more important part of Intel’s business. Company leaders, including Andy Grove and Gordon Moore, confronted the question of whether to keep defending the original product category or redirect resources. Intel gradually withdrew from dynamic random-access memory and concentrated more heavily on microprocessors. The shift affected factories, product priorities, staffing, and the company’s identity. The microprocessor strategy later became central to Intel’s growth during the personal-computer era. The case is often discussed as a strategic pivot, but it was also a workplace decision involving what skills, investments, and habits the organization would continue to follow. Adaptation required letting go of a business that had once defined the company. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

The microprocessor strategy later became central to Intel’s growth during the personal-computer era. The case is often discussed as a strategic pivot, but it was also a workplace decision involving what skills, investments, and habits the organization would continue to follow. Adaptation required letting go of a business that had once defined the company. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Historical event Vera Wang shifts from skating and publishing to fashion. Vera Wang trained seriously as a figure skater and competed at a high level, but she did not make the U.S. Olympic team. She then moved into fashion publishing and spent many years at Vogue before working at Ralph Lauren. When planning her own wedding, she became dissatisfied with available bridal options and eventually entered bridal design. In 1990 she opened a bridal boutique in New York, first carrying established designers and then developing her own collections. The business expanded into a global fashion brand. The path was gradual and built on skills from earlier stages: discipline from sport, visual judgment from editorial work, and industry knowledge from fashion companies. Historical case EUUS-WL-H52: Martha Stewart turns a catering business into a media career · Connecticut and New York, United States · 1970s–1990s

307 Yao H52 Gen Line 1▶
307爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H52-L1 | XIANG-L-52-02 | Record 307

Historical event Martha Stewart turns a catering business into a media career. Martha Stewart worked in modeling and then as a stockbroker before moving with her family to Connecticut. She developed skills in cooking, entertaining, gardening, and home renovation while building a local catering business. The catering work led to publishing opportunities. Her first book, Entertaining, appeared in 1982, followed by additional books, television appearances, a magazine, and eventually Martha Stewart Living Omnimedia. The business connected practical domestic activities with publishing, retail, licensing, and broadcast media. Her later career included serious legal and corporate setbacks, followed by continued business activity after imprisonment. The earlier transition is useful as a life-and-work case because it turned activities often treated as private household labor into a structured professional platform. The new career emerged from a different status relationship than her earlier finance job and required redefining what counted as marketable expertise. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

309 Yao H52 Gen Line 3▶
309爻 Line 3 | TW-WL-H52-L3 | XIANG-L-52-04 | Record 309

Historical event Martha Stewart turns a catering business into a media career. Martha Stewart worked in modeling and then as a stockbroker before moving with her family to Connecticut. She developed skills in cooking, entertaining, gardening, and home renovation while building a local catering business. The catering work led to publishing opportunities. Her first book, Entertaining, appeared in 1982, followed by additional books, television appearances, a magazine, and eventually Martha Stewart Living Omnimedia. The business connected practical domestic activities with publishing, retail, licensing, and broadcast media. Her later career included serious legal and corporate setbacks, followed by continued business activity after imprisonment. The earlier transition is useful as a life-and-work case because it turned activities often treated as private household labor into a structured professional platform. The new career emerged from a different status relationship than her earlier finance job and required redefining what counted as marketable expertise. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H53: Dolly Parton launches the Imagination Library · Tennessee, United States · 1995 onward

313 Yao H53 Jian Line 1▶
313爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H53-L1 | XIANG-L-53-02 | Record 313

Historical event Dolly Parton launches the Imagination Library. Dolly Parton had built a successful music and entertainment career before creating the Imagination Library in 1995 through the Dollywood Foundation. The program began in her home county in Tennessee and was inspired in part by her father’s limited formal education. The program mails age-appropriate books to enrolled children from birth until school age. It expanded through partnerships with local communities that share the work and funding needed to operate the program in different regions. Over time it spread across the United States and into other countries. The project became a large literacy initiative independent of any single album or performance cycle. It demonstrates abundance redirected toward a recurring public benefit: celebrity, organizational capacity, and financial resources were used to build an institution that functions through distributed local participation. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. Historical case EUUS-WL-H54: Mark Twain leaves riverboating and builds a writing career · United States · 1860s

319 Yao H54 Gui Mei Line 1▶
319爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H54-L1 | XIANG-L-54-02 | Record 319

Historical event Mark Twain leaves riverboating and builds a writing career. Samuel Clemens trained and worked as a Mississippi River pilot before the American Civil War disrupted commercial river traffic. The occupation had required technical knowledge, navigation, and a specific regional economy. He traveled west, tried mining, worked in journalism, and began publishing humorous sketches under the name Mark Twain. Reporting assignments and travel supplied material for books, and his lecture career added another source of income and public visibility. Writing gradually replaced his earlier occupational identity. Twain became one of the best-known American authors, but he also experienced business failures and financial problems later in life. The traveler theme is practical: moving through places and jobs did not guarantee success, yet each environment supplied observations, contacts, and material that made a different career possible. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Historical case EUUS-WL-H55: IKEA standardizes flat-pack furniture · Sweden and Europe · 1950s onward

325 Yao H55 Feng Line 1▶
325爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H55-L1 | XIANG-L-55-02 | Record 325

Historical event IKEA standardizes flat-pack furniture. Ingvar Kamprad founded IKEA in Sweden as a mail-order business and gradually focused it on furniture. Shipping assembled furniture was expensive and vulnerable to damage, limiting how efficiently products could reach customers. During the 1950s IKEA increasingly adopted flat-pack designs that customers could transport and assemble themselves. Designers worked within constraints of packaging, manufacturing, cost, and simple assembly. The company paired this with catalogs, large self-service stores, standardized product names, and a retail experience that explained the system to customers. The model expanded internationally and became closely associated with IKEA’s identity. Its influence was not based on one dramatic invention but on persistent design decisions that penetrated many parts of the business. Small operational choices reinforced one another until the whole customer experience worked differently. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

The model expanded internationally and became closely associated with IKEA’s identity. Its influence was not based on one dramatic invention but on persistent design decisions that penetrated many parts of the business. Small operational choices reinforced one another until the whole customer experience worked differently. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

337 Yao H57 Xun Line 1▶
337爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H57-L1 | XIANG-L-57-02 | Record 337

Historical event Automattic builds a distributed company. Automattic was founded around WordPress-related products and from an early stage hired people who worked from many locations rather than from a single headquarters. Distributed work was unusual for a growing technology company in the mid-2000s. The company developed written communication practices, online collaboration tools, periodic in-person gatherings, and hiring processes designed for remote work. Employees could live in different countries and time zones, which broadened recruiting but also increased the need for documentation and asynchronous coordination. Automattic grew into a large distributed organization and became a frequently cited example of remote-first work. The model is not free of tradeoffs; it requires deliberate communication and can make informal coordination harder. The case illustrates dispersion managed through common systems rather than through physical proximity. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. Distributed work was unusual for a growing technology company in the mid-2000s. The company developed written communication practices, online collaboration tools, periodic in-person gatherings, and hiring processes designed for remote work. Employees could live in different countries and time zones, which broadened recruiting but also increased the need for documentation and asynchronous coordination. Automattic grew into a large distributed organization and became a frequently cited example of remote-first work. The model is not free of tradeoffs; it requires deliberate communication and can make informal coordination harder. The case illustrates dispersion managed through common systems rather than through physical proximity. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H58: Basecamp formalizes a forty-hour workweek culture · United States · 2000s–2010s

343 Yao H58 Dui Line 1▶
343爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H58-L1 | XIANG-L-58-02 | Record 343

Historical event Basecamp formalizes a forty-hour workweek culture. The software company 37signals, later known as Basecamp, built products with a relatively small team and publicly argued against the idea that long hours were a necessary sign of commitment in technology work. Its leaders wrote about limiting meetings, reducing simultaneous projects, using fixed cycles, and maintaining roughly forty-hour workweeks, with shorter weeks in some summer periods. These practices were tied to product choices that favored smaller teams and limited scope rather than constant expansion. The company remained private and profitable for many years, though its culture and policies also became controversial during later internal disputes. The workplace decision is about limitation: a boundary around hours and project load can support focus, but only if the organization also limits what it promises customers and itself. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H59: Cadbury develops Bournville as a worker community · Birmingham, Britain · late 1800s–early 1900s

349 Yao H59 Huan Line 1▶
349爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H59-L1 | XIANG-L-59-02 | Record 349

Historical event Cadbury develops Bournville as a worker community. Cadbury expanded chocolate manufacturing near Birmingham during the nineteenth century. As the business grew, George and Richard Cadbury moved production to a less crowded site at Bournville, where there was room for a larger factory and a different environment around it. The company and related trusts developed housing, green space, recreation, and community facilities for workers and residents. The model reflected Quaker social ideas as well as practical concerns about health, stability, and the conditions of industrial employment. It was still a company-centered environment, and later observers have debated the balance between welfare and paternalism. Bournville influenced discussions of model villages and employer responsibility. The case is useful for inner truth because stated values were made visible in physical investment. Credibility depended on whether workers could experience the policy in housing, sanitation, recreation, and workplace conditions rather than only in company language. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical event Cadbury develops Bournville as a worker community. Cadbury expanded chocolate manufacturing near Birmingham during the nineteenth century. The model reflected Quaker social ideas as well as practical concerns about health, stability, and the conditions of industrial employment. Historical case EUUS-WL-H60: Southwest Airlines grows through a focused route model · United States · 1971 onward

355 Yao H60 Jie Line 1▶
355爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H60-L1 | XIANG-L-60-02 | Record 355

Historical event Southwest Airlines grows through a focused route model. Southwest Airlines began service in Texas in 1971 after years of legal challenges from competing airlines. It entered a regulated industry with fewer resources than major carriers and initially focused on a small network of short routes. The airline emphasized quick aircraft turnaround, standardized fleets, point-to-point service, and simple fares. Growth came by adding routes and cities while trying to preserve operating routines that supported low costs. Expansion accelerated after U.S. airline deregulation, but the company generally avoided becoming a traditional hub-and-spoke network. Southwest became a major U.S. airline and was profitable for many years, though later operational disruptions and industry changes exposed weaknesses that required further investment. The historical growth pattern shows “small exceeding” as bounded expansion: doing many small operational things consistently before attempting every possible market. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H61: Apollo 11 team returns from the Moon and shifts to follow-on work · United States · 1969 onward

361 Yao H61 Zhong Fu Line 1▶
361爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H61-L1 | XIANG-L-61-02 | Record 361

Historical event Apollo 11 team returns from the Moon and shifts to follow-on work. Apollo 11 achieved the first crewed lunar landing in July 1969 after years of engineering, testing, accidents, budget commitments, and work by a very large NASA and contractor workforce. The landing fulfilled the central public objective set for the Apollo program earlier in the decade. After the mission, Neil Armstrong, Buzz Aldrin, Michael Collins, flight controllers, engineers, and managers moved into different assignments. NASA still had additional Apollo missions, scientific goals, hardware decisions, and institutional questions to manage. The achievement did not eliminate risk, as later missions such as Apollo 13 demonstrated. Apollo 11 became a symbol of completion, but the program’s work continued and eventually ended after Apollo 17 in 1972. The case illustrates a common workplace problem after success: a milestone can create the illusion that the system no longer needs vigilance. Completion changes the task; it does not abolish the need for maintenance, learning, and decisions about what comes next. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward.

Historical event Apollo 11 team returns from the Moon and shifts to follow-on work. Historical case EUUS-WL-H62: Jeff Bezos leaves finance to start Amazon · United States · 1994 onward

367 Yao H62 Xiao Guo Line 1▶
367爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H62-L1 | XIANG-L-62-02 | Record 367

Historical event Jeff Bezos leaves finance to start Amazon. Jeff Bezos worked in finance and technology roles in New York, including at the hedge fund D. E. Shaw. In the early 1990s he observed rapid growth in internet usage and considered business models that could take advantage of online distribution. In 1994 he left his job and moved to the Seattle area, where he founded Amazon as an online bookseller. The company began with a narrow category that benefited from a vast catalog and mail delivery, then expanded into other products, marketplaces, cloud computing, devices, and logistics. Growth required repeated reinvestment and tolerance for years in which profits were limited relative to expansion. Amazon became one of the largest companies in the world, while its labor practices, market power, and competitive effects remain subjects of debate. The initial life decision illustrates “before completion”: evidence can be strong enough to justify beginning even when the final business model is unknown. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

Historical case EUUS-WL-H63: Susan Wojcicki joins Google and builds its advertising business · California, United States · 1998–2014

373 Yao H63 Ji Ji Line 1▶
373爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H63-L1 | XIANG-L-63-02 | Record 373

Historical event Susan Wojcicki joins Google and builds its advertising business. Susan Wojcicki was working in technology and business roles when Larry Page and Sergey Brin rented part of her Menlo Park home as an early Google workspace. The connection put her close to a young company whose commercial model was still unsettled. She joined Google in 1999 as an early marketing manager and later led advertising and analytics products, including work around AdWords and AdSense. She also advocated the acquisition of YouTube and later became YouTube chief executive in 2014. Her career expanded through roles that linked user growth, advertising economics, product strategy, and acquisitions. Wojcicki led YouTube until 2023 and remained associated with Google and Alphabet afterward. Historical case EUUS-WL-H64: Tim Berners-Lee helps keep the World Wide Web open · Geneva, Europe and United States · 1989 onward

379 Yao H64 Wei Ji Line 1▶
379爻 Line 1 | TW-WL-H64-L1 | XIANG-L-64-02 | Record 379

Historical event Tim Berners-Lee helps keep the World Wide Web open. Tim Berners-Lee worked at CERN, where researchers needed better ways to share information across different computers and institutions. In 1989 he proposed a hypertext system that became the foundation of the World Wide Web. He developed core technologies including HTML, HTTP, and the first web server and browser. CERN later released the web software into the public domain, allowing others to build compatible sites and tools without paying licensing fees. Berners-Lee then moved to MIT and founded the World Wide Web Consortium to coordinate open standards. The web expanded far beyond the environment in which it was created and became basic infrastructure for communication, commerce, publishing, and software. The decision to support open standards did not settle every later problem of privacy, competition, or platform power. It left the system deliberately unfinished so that others could extend it. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.

This is a retrospective workplace and life-decision reading, not evidence that the people in the case consulted the Ten Wings. The Small Image emphasizes: “Their tail gets immersed: this is the very height of ignorance.” Read that image as a prompt about the opening stage, when uncertainty is cheapest to examine. In the documented case, Tim Berners-Lee worked at CERN, where researchers needed better ways to share information across different computers and institutions. The useful parallel is not a claim of textual influence; it is the way the sequence makes a practical decision problem visible. Separate what is controllable from what is fixed, identify who else bears the consequences, and define what evidence would change the plan. What early signal deserves attention before commitment grows? A good next step should be specific enough to test, limited enough to revise, and clear enough that colleagues or family members can understand the tradeoff. Use the line as a short review prompt: name the decision, the constraint, the stakeholder, and the next checkpoint. The historical source documents the event only; this comparison is a contemporary editorial analogy.

384 Yao H64 Wei Ji Line 6▶
384爻 Line 6 | TW-WL-H64-L6 | XIANG-L-64-07 | Record 384

Historical event Tim Berners-Lee helps keep the World Wide Web open. Tim Berners-Lee worked at CERN, where researchers needed better ways to share information across different computers and institutions. In 1989 he proposed a hypertext system that became the foundation of the World Wide Web. He developed core technologies including HTML, HTTP, and the first web server and browser. CERN later released the web software into the public domain, allowing others to build compatible sites and tools without paying licensing fees. Berners-Lee then moved to MIT and founded the World Wide Web Consortium to coordinate open standards. The web expanded far beyond the environment in which it was created and became basic infrastructure for communication, commerce, publishing, and software. The decision to support open standards did not settle every later problem of privacy, competition, or platform power. It left the system deliberately unfinished so that others could extend it. Seen as a workplace and life-decision story, the important sequence is practical rather than dramatic. The situation created a constraint or opening; a person or team made a choice within the resources and institutions available; the result then created new responsibilities rather than a final ending. The record does not require invented dialogue or private motives to make the decision understandable. It can be followed through observable actions, roles, projects, and later consequences. The case also shows why career and life decisions are rarely isolated from context. Skills, money, family obligations, colleagues, technology, rules, and timing shape what options are realistically available. The decision can therefore be described without claiming that success was inevitable. What matters historically is the chain from the conditions before the choice, through the work required to carry it out, to the consequences that became visible afterward. Later reputation can make the result look obvious, but it was not obvious at the beginning. The value of the episode lies in the documented sequence of action and consequence, not in turning the person into a flawless model.
